L1315: ## 13.2.8. Excipients
L1316: ## Isomalt(b) (4)
L1317: Isomalt(b) (4) at maximum daily exposure (MDE) [REDACTED] mg/day exceeds levels documented in the FDA Inactive Ingredients Database (IID) of MDE of [REDACTED] mg/day. The Applicant
L1318: provided safety justification stating isomalt is regarded as a nontoxic, nonallergenic, and non-irritant with no known mutagenic or carcinogenic potential and no effects on reproduction or embryofetal development; and tolerated at 10% in diet for long term in several toxicological species. The Applicant provided published information indicating that daily exposure in rats that weigh 0.25 to 0.5 kg that consumed a diet containing isomalt at a dose of (b)(4) g/kg/day supported a human equivalent dose up to (b)(4) g/day for 50 kg person, which is (b)(4)-fold higher than MDE of isomalt ((b)(4) mg/day) in patients, which is considered acceptable.
L1319: ## **Hypromellose** (b)(4)
L1320: Hypromellose (b)(4) is not listed in IID, but Center for Drug Evaluation and Research/Office of Pharmaceutical Quality considers this same as (b)(4). Hypromellose is in the IID at MDE of (b)(4) mg/day and qualifies the exposure from drug at MDE of (b)(4) mg/day.
L1321: ## **Strawberry Flavor**
L1322: The Applicant describes the Strawberry Flavor as being composed of several sub-ingredients, and justifies the levels based on a statement provided by (b)(4) to suggest this flavor is in use in powdered medication at a flavor concentration of (b)(4) with MDE of (b)(4) mg/day in patients aged 6 to 12 years, and at (b)(4) mg/day in patients &gt;13 years of age. They state that the levels are much lower in levacetylleucine ((b)(4) mg/day). Further, the Applicant describes this flavor is composed of (b)(4).
L1323: Overall, the ingredients in this flavoring appear acceptable based on available safety data.

L1330: ## 14. Clinical Pharmacology
L1331: ## 14.1. In Vitro Studies
L1332: ## Protein Binding
L1333: The plasma protein binding of levacetylleucine was assessed in plasma from CD1 mice, Sprague-Dawley rats, New Zealand White rabbits, Beagle dogs, and humans at concentrations of 1 µg/mL, 10 µg/mL, and 100 µg/mL using rapid equilibrium dialysis (Study No. ADM-20-2970b, 2020). The analysis was performed using a specially developed liquid chromatography with tandem mass spectrometry method, with all samples compared against a calibration curve prepared in a corresponding matrix. The results showed that the free fractions of levacetylleucine were approximately 100% across all species and concentrations, indicating minimal binding to plasma proteins.
L1334: ## Metabolism
L1335: ## Study No. ADM-20-2970a, 2020
L1336: In this study, a deuterated N-acetyl-DL-leucine was used to compare metabolism across various species (human, mouse, rat, rabbit, dog) at concentrations of 40 µM, 2mM, and 1, 10, and 100µM. The study found that only one metabolite, L-leucine, was produced during a 1-hour incubation period, regardless of the species. The highest metabolic turnover of N-acetyl-DL-leucine-D10 was observed in rabbit liver S9, with 39 to 51% of the initial concentration remaining after 60 minutes. In comparison, mouse, rat, and human liver S9 showed a remaining concentration range of 47 to 72%. The lowest disappearance rate was seen in dog liver S9, where 85% or more of the parent molecule remained after 60 minutes. The disappearance of N-acetyl-DL-leucine-D10 was not cofactor-dependent, indicating it was not mediated by cytochrome P450 (CYP), uridine diphosphate glucuronosyltransferase, or sulfotransferase enzymes.
L1337: No qualitative differences in metabolism were observed between the two doses across any species, indicating consistent metabolic capacity across species. The measured concentrations of L-leucine-D10 correlated well with the disappearance of N-acetyl-DL-leucine-D10, suggesting that the observed disappearance is primarily due to the formation of L-leucine-D10.
L1338: ## Excretion
L1339: No specific excretion studies have been conducted for levacetylleucine. However, since its metabolism produces only acetate and L-leucine, both of which are further processed in the body through catabolic pathways, dedicated excretion studies were deemed unnecessary. Lee et al. 2015 showed that after administering an intravenous dose of ^{14}\text{C}-L-leucine to rats, 5% of the parent compound was excreted in urine as levacetylleucine.
L1340: ## Cytochrome P450 Induction
L1341: ## Study No. ADM-18-2066a, 2018
L1342: The induction potential of levacetylleucine on human CYP enzymes 1A2, 2B6, and 3A4 at the mRNA level was evaluated using cryopreserved hepatocytes from three human donors at concentrations ranging from 6 to 2000 µg/mL [Study No. ADM-18-2066a, 2018]. The total
L1343: exposure duration was 48 hours, with the cell culture media containing the test compound being refreshed after 24 hours. Cytotoxicity was assessed visually and by measuring lactate dehydrogenase leakage after 24 hours of incubation.
L1344: The mRNA levels of CYP1A2, CYP2B6, and CYP3A4 following incubation with levacetylleucine at concentrations of 6 to 2000 µg/mL remained within 0.5- to 1.7-fold of the solvent control and below 3% of the corresponding positive control inducer. These results indicate that N-acetyl-L-leucine did not induce these genes. Additionally, no signs of cytotoxicity or morphological changes were observed due to the test compound.
L1345: ## Transporter Inhibition
L1346: ## P-gp, BCRP, and BSEP
L1347: ## Study No. ADM-18-2106a, 2018
L1348: Efflux transporter inhibition was assessed by measuring the vesicular uptake of a probe substrate into inside-out membrane vesicles expressing individual human adenosine triphosphate (ATP) binding cassette transporters (P-glycoprotein [P-gp], breast cancer resistance protein [BCRP], and bile salt export pump [BSEP]) in the presence and absence of levacetylleucine (MW=173.21 g/mol) at six concentrations ranging from 6 µg/mL to 2000 µg/mL, both with and without ATP [Study No. ADM-18-2106a, 2018]. Inhibition of the transporters was indicated by a reduced level of vesicular uptake of the probe substrate in the presence of ATP.
L1349: The Applicant demonstrated that levacetylleucine inhibited the efflux transporters P-gp, BCRP, and BSEP, with IC50 values of 227 µg/mL (1.31mM) for P-gp, 491 µg/mL (2.83mM) for BCRP, and 408 µg/mL (2.36mM) for BSEP.
L1350: Based on the molecular weight of levacetylleucine (173.21 g/mol) and its IC50 values for key efflux transporters, potential drug interactions have been assessed. With a maximum dose of 2 grams, the calculated Igut/IC50 ratio is close to 10 for BCRP, but a very high ratio of 35.2 for P-gp was observed. This ratio suggests a potential for interaction with substrates of P-gp. The FDA has recommended a PMR study to further investigate these potential interactions.
L1351: ## OATP1B1*1, OATP1B3, OAT1, OAT3, and OCT2
L1352: ## Study No. ADM-18-2106c, 2018
L1353: Human solute carrier transporter inhibition was evaluated by measuring the cellular uptake of probe substrates into HEK293 cells (Corning® TransportoCells™) overexpressing a single human solute carrier transporter (organic anion transporter [OAT]P1B1*1, OATP1B3, OAT1, OAT3, or organic cation transporter [OCT]2) in the absence and presence of levacetylleucine (MW=173.21 g/mol) at concentrations ranging from 30 to 900 µg/mL [Study No. ADM-18-2106c, 2018]. Inhibition of OATP1B1 and OATP1B3 was evaluated both with and without a 30-minute pre-incubation of the cells with the test compound. Inhibition of active transport was indicated by decreased cellular uptake of the probe substrate in the presence of the inhibitor. Positive control inhibitors for each transporter were incubated in parallel to confirm active cellular uptake of the probe substrate.
L1354: Inhibition of OAT1 and OAT3 by levacetylleucine was observed, with IC50 values of 1030 µg/mL (5.9mM) for OAT1 and 103 µg/mL (0.59mM) for OAT3. Additionally, minor inhibition of OCT2 was observed, with 61% activity remaining at 900 µg/mL
L1355: NDA 219132
Aqneursa (levacetylleucine)
L1356: levacetylleucine (extrapolated IC_{50} of 1700 µg/mL). Levacetylleucine was not an inhibitor of OATP1B1*1 or OATP1B3.
L1357: Based on these results, inhibition of the human solute carrier transporters OATP1B1*1, OATP1B3, OAT1, OAT3, or OCT2 by levacetylleucine is considered unlikely.
L1358: ## Transporter Substrate
L1359: ## **P-gp and BCRP**
L1360: The apparent permeability of levacetylleucine was measured bidirectionally (pH 7.4; apical to basolateral [A&gt;B] and basolateral to apical [B&gt;A]) at a concentration of 10 µg/mL through Madin-Darby canine kidney (MDCK)-wild type, MDCK-BCRP, and MDCK-P-gp cell monolayers. Incubations with MDCK-BCRP and MDCK-P-gp cells were conducted both in the presence and absence of specific chemical inhibitors for BCRP and P-gp, respectively [Study No. ADM-18-2106d, 2018]. Appropriate positive and negative controls were included. Transporter inhibition was indicated by a reduced level of vesicular uptake of the probe substrate in the presence of ATP.
L1361: The efflux ratio for levacetylleucine was 1.6 with MDCK-wild type cells (consistent across both plates), 0.7 with MDCK-BCRP without inhibitor and 1.3 with the inhibitor (2 µM Ko143), and 1.6 with MDCK-P-gp without inhibitor and 2.6 with the inhibitor (50µM verapamil). Therefore, no BCRP- or P-gp-mediated active transport of N-acetyl-L-leucine was detected in this study.
L1362: ## **OAT1, OAT3, and OCT2**
L1363: The cellular uptake of levacetylleucine was measured in human cells transfected with single transporters at two concentrations (10 and 100 µg/mL) both in the presence and absence of chemical transporter inhibitors, as well as in control cells without transfected transporters and without inhibitors [Study No. ADM-18-2106e, 2018]. Transporter-mediated uptake was identified by comparing the cellular uptake in transporter overexpressing cells to that in vector-transfected control cells and by observing the inhibition of uptake in transporter overexpressing cells when control inhibitors were used. Positive control substrates at a single concentration were tested in parallel.
L1364: Levacetylleucine was found to be a substrate for OAT1 and OAT3, but not OCT2, in vitro. Uptake into OAT1-expressing cells was approximately 5-fold higher, and into OAT3-expressing cells approximately 9-fold higher, after 3 minutes of incubation compared to control cells. This uptake was completely inhibited by the addition of diclofenac.
L1365: The clinical relevance of this finding is unknown. However, due to the overall safety profile of levacetylleucine, no significant drug interactions with inhibitors of OAT1 or OAT3 transporters are expected.
L1366: ## 14.2. In Vivo Studies
L1367: ## Trial IB1001-201
L1368: Pharmacokinetic (PK) analysis for Trial IB1001-201 was conducted on the PK set, which included all subjects who received at least one dose of levacetylleucine in the extension phase (EP) and had at least one PK plasma concentration measurement after oral administration. Intensive PK samples were collected at Visit 7B, following the first administration of the drug, and again after 1 year of treatment (Visit 9B). The Applicant conducted non-
L1369: compartment analysis using the intensive PK data. Sparse PK sampling from the parent study was not included in this context but was instead used for the population PK (PopPK) analysis.
L1370: At Visit 7B, the highest mean plasma concentration of the drug was observed 1 hour after administration, with a mean (\pmstandard deviation [SD]) C_{max} value of 6.69 (\pm1.85) \mug/mL, and a C_{max} value of 10.04 \mug/mL (Table 63). At Visit 9B, the highest mean plasma concentration was again reached 1-hour post-treatment, with a mean (\pmSD) C_{max} value of 8.32 (\pm3.3 SD) \mug/mL and a C_{max} value of 15.85 \mug/mL (Table 64). However, geometric coefficient of variation values at all time points exceeded 40%, indicating substantial variability in plasma concentrations among subjects.
L1371: When administered orally, levacetylleucine was rapidly absorbed, with time to maximum concentration values ranging from 0.5 to 2.5 hours and a median value of 1 hour. The half-life (t_{1/2}) was approximately 1 hour. Likely metabolites include L-leucine and acetic acid. Elimination of levacetylleucine from plasma was rapid after a single dose. The apparent volume of distribution was 200 L, and the apparent clearance was 144 L/h. Similar values were observed at steady state. A large inter-individual variability (coefficient of variation: 40 to 50%) was observed at both Visit 7B and Visit 9B. No relevant sex differences were observed in any of the PK parameters. PK parameters for visit 7B are summarized descriptively in (Table 63) and PK parameters for visit 9B are summarized descriptively in (Table 64).

L1390: ## Trial IB1001-301 – PK Results
L1391: At the time when action was taken on initial NDA submission, the IB1001-301 EP is still ongoing, and therefore data from the rich PK sampling are not yet available. No noncompartmental PK analysis was performed on data from the sparse PK sampling from the parent study (Trial IB1001-301 PK report); instead, these samples were utilized in the PopPK analysis.
L1392: ## Impact of Food Effect on Pharmacokinetics
L1393: The Applicant did not conduct dedicated food effect study using the to-be-marketed formulation. In Trial IB1001-301, drug administration was proposed to be under fasted conditions (at least 30 minutes before meal or at least 2 hours after meal). However, approximately 12.5% non-compliance with fasting was reported. The food components (high
L1394: fat versus low fat) were unknown. The review team conducted preliminary PK analyses to compare the drug concentration at various time points (0.5-, 1-, 1.5-, and 2-hour) following drug administrations under two scenarios: with and without meal intake. The results (Figure 23 and Table 67) show that the drug concentrations are similar between the groups that consumed a meal and those that did not across all time points. While there is some variability within each group, the overall pattern indicates that meal intake did not significantly affect drug concentration during the first 2 hours postdose. These data suggest that given the current available information, the drug's absorption and subsequent concentrations remain similar regardless of meal intake.

L1401: ## 14.3. Bioanalytical Method Validation and Performance
L1402: A high-performance liquid chromatography method with tandem mass spectrometric was developed (b) (4) for quantifying N-acetyl-L-leucine in human plasma. This method was used to analyze plasma samples from clinical Trials IB1001-202 (phase II), IB1001-203 (phase II), and IB1001-301 (phase III).

L1414: **Table 70. Summary of Method Performance of a Bioanalytical Method Determination of N-Acetyl-L-Leucine in Human Plasma (Part 2)**
L1415: Method Summary
Method description	Internally standardized HPLC method using reverse-phase chromatography with a CHIRALPAK QD-AX column and tandem mass spectrometric detection using a Sciex API 4000 Qtrap mass spectrometer. Plasma samples spiked with the internal standard (N-acetyl-DL-leucine- d10) and acetonitrile are enriched with methanol and analyzed by HPLC-MS/MS. Run time (time between injections) was approximately 8.0 minutes.
Materials used for standard calibration curve and concentration	N-acetyl-L-leucine stock solution (10000.000 µg/mL) was prepared in methanol. Stock solution was further diluted to spiking solutions with 50:50 methanol:water (2.000-2000.000 µg/mL)
Spiking solutions were spiked in pooled human plasma to prepare the calibration standards.
Concentration of calibration standards: 0.100, 0.200, 0.500, 1.000, 10.000, 50.000, 80.000 and 100.000 µg/mL
Validated assay range	0.100 to 100.000 µg/mL
Material used for quality controls (QCs) and concentration	A separate N-acetyl-L-leucine stock solution (10000.000 µg/mL) was prepared in methanol. Stock solution was further diluted to spiking solutions with 50:50 methanol:water (2.000-1500.000 µg/mL)
Spiking solutions were spiked in pooled human plasma to prepare quality controls.
Concentration of quality controls (QCs): 0.100 (QC lower limit), 0.300 (QC low), 30.000 (QC medium), 75.000 (QC high) and 1000.000 (QC upper limit) µg/mL
Minimum required dilution (MRD)	Not applicable
Source and lot of reagents	Calibrator standard: N-acetyl-L-leucine, lot number LT 40, used for preparation of calibration, validation and QC samples, provided by (b) (4)
Internal standard: N-acetyl-DL-leucine-d10, Lot number AZB 4572 a36 IV, used as internal standard, provided by (b) (4)
Biological Matrix: Blank human, fresh frozen lithium heparin plasma, provided by (b) (4), was used for QC samples and calibrators (Lot number: BRH 1565746-55), for measuring matrix effect (Lots number: BRH 1565747 – BRH 1565752), for measuring lipemic effect (Lot number: BRH 1565744), for measuring hemolytic effect (Lot number: BRH 1565745) and for measuring selectivity (Lots number: BRH 1565746 – BRH 1565755)
Regression model and weighting	Sample concentrations were determined mathematically comparing the peak area ratios in the sample to the weighted (1/x) regression equation obtained from the standards data

L1417: Abbreviations: HPLC = high-performance liquid chromatography; QC = quality controls

Selectivity & matrix effect	Selectivity of the method was tested using 10 different human plasma lots. Ten out of ten processed blank samples without internal standard (IS) and with IS showed negligible interfering signal with N-acetyl-L-leucine. The method is selective for N-acetyl-L-leucine analytes.
Matrix effect of the method was tested using 6 different human plasma lots. The CV of the IS- normalized matrix factor (MF) at QC low, QC medium and QC high level is <15 % and therefore in good accordance with the acceptance criteria
Recovery*	Recovery values of the analyte calculated comparing the detector response of N-acetyl-L-leucine and internal standard to the response of the analyte in spiked blank extracts are very consistent for the matrix samples
Interference & specificity	Interference between N-acetyl-L-leucine and N-acetyl-DL-leucine-d10 were tested using three ULOQ without IS samples and three blanks with IS with one lot of plasma. No interference from N-acetyl-DL-leucine-d10 to N-acetyl-L-leucine, as well as from N-acetyl-L-leucine to N-acetyl-DL-leucine-d10 were detected.
Hemolysis effect	Hemolysis effect in three lots were tested, the bias for all hemolytic plasma samples was within acceptance criteria

L1466: ## 14.5. Pharmacometrics Assessment
L1467: ## Population PK Analysis
L1468: ## Applicant's Population PK Analysis
L1469: The PopPK analysis for levacetylleucine was conducted based on the PK data collected from three trials: IB1001-201 (n=32), IB1001-202 (n=25), and IB1001-301 (n=38). Sparse PK sampling was collected in Trials IB1001-201, IB1001-202 and IB1001-301 and rich PK sampling was conducted in the EPs of Trials IB1001-201 and IB1001-202. A total of 567 levacetylleucine concentrations from 95 subjects were used in PopPK analysis. In the PopPK population, majority of subjects were White (94%) and male (51%). The median (range) age was 26 (6, 67) years old, and the median (range) body weight was 59.2 (20.5, 98.4) kg. Dosing regimen used in IB1001-201 is same as the proposed dosing regimen with minor deviations in the age categories for dosing in IB1001-201 and IB1001-202.
L1470: Phoenix WinNonlinTM nonlinear mixed effect using estimation method of First Order Conditional Estimation-Extended Least Squares (FOCE ELS) was used for PopPK analysis. To investigate the source of between-subject variability, intrinsic factors and extrinsic factors were tested for inclusion in the base model using forward stepwise covariate search to test the addition or deletion of covariates effects by adding one at a time. Visual predictive check and bootstrap test were used to validate the final covariate model.
L1471: ## NDA 219132
## Aqneursa (levacetylleucine)
L1472: The base model is a one-compartment model with the error model combining additive and multiplicative method. The base model also includes a 5-minute lag time (T_{lag}) and covariance between clearance (CL) and volume of distribution (V). Between-subject variability coefficient of variation were 39% for CL and 74% for V. The examined covariates were age, sex, weight, body mass index, body surface area, disease (NPC or GM2 gangliosidosis), race (White, Asian or other), dose (1 gram or 2 grams), miglustat as comedication, and meal consumption (no meal, meal consumed during the 2 hours before drug administration, meal consumed during the 2 hours after drug administration, meal consumed during the 2 hours both before and after the drug administration). The final PopPK model includes the effect of body weight with power model on CL and V, and the effect of dose (1 gram versus 2 grams) on the absorption rate constant (K_a). The final model estimates are presented in Table 82. Goodness-of-fit plots and visual predictive check are presented in Figure 24 and Figure 25. Between-subject variability coefficient of variation were 29% for CL, 80% for V, and 19% for K_a. Eta shrinkages were 20%, 44%, and 48% for CL, V, and K_a.

L1482: ## Reviewer's Assessment
L1483: Data handling of exclusions and correction of missing values and implausible values were adequately documented. The final PopPK model reasonably describes the observed PK data. The reviewer's independent analysis shows similar parameter estimates as Applicant's. The structural parameters were estimated with acceptable precision (relative standard error &lt;20%). The goodness-of-fit plots does not show obvious model misspecification, and the visual predictive check plots shows that the model generally captures the central tendency and variability of the observed data. No systemic trends were observed in ETA-covariate plots (Figure 26). Age was not identified as significant covariate once body weight is included in the model. Eta shrinkage for CL less than 20% supports use of empiric Bayes estimates to derive individual CL and exposure metrics of AUC. Given the fast absorption with a short half-life and the moderately high eta shrinkage for V, the model derived Cmax was only used for the purpose of comparisons with the model derived Cmax values predicted for different body weight groups when evaluating dosing regimens.
L1484: Figure 26. ETA-Covariate Relationship (ETA1: Eta for CL, ETA2: Eta for V)
L1485: Source: Reviewer's analysis
L1486: Abbreviations: 0 = Yes, 1 = No; 2 = NA; BSA = body surface area; SEXF = sex, 0-male and 1-female; COVDIS = disease as covariate, 1-NPC and 2-GM2; CL = clearance; COVMIG = comedication with miglustat; V = volume of distribution
L1487: Applicant's PopPK analysis assumed that levacetylleucine follows dose-linear PK. There is no dose-proportionality information provided by Applicant. The available PK data used for PopPK analysis are limited to evaluate relative oral bioavailability (1 gram versus 2 grams). The subjects younger than 13 years old and weighing &lt;35 kg received 1 gram at the time of PK sampling and the remaining subjects (\ge13 years old or \ge35 kg) received 2 grams at the time of PK sampling. Since PK samples were collected only after the morning dose, there are no data where two dose strengths (1 gram and 2 grams) were tested within a subject or in similar subjects in terms of body size (e.g., weight) at the time of PK sampling. This exclusive correlation between body
L1488: weight and dose levels does not allow covariate analysis to assess independent impacts of body size (e.g., weight) and dose level (1 gram versus 2 grams) on PK parameters, such as apparent clearance and relative bioavailability. If dose-proportionality data become available in the future, the PopPK model should be re-assessed accordingly.
L1489: (b) (4)
L1490: ## Exposure-Response Analysis
L1491: Reviewer conducted E-R analysis for Agency-recommended rescored scale for the assessment and rating of ataxia (SARA) (functional SARA) based on the data from 38 subjects who had both efficacy evaluation and evaluable PK data in Trial IB1001-301. The exposure metric used for E-R analysis was daily AUC at steady state derived based on the individual predicted PK parameters (empirical bayes estimates) following the dosing regimen received during phase 3 study. No apparent E-R relationship for efficacy endpoint was observed (Figure 27). Due to the limited event rate for each treatment-emergent adverse event (Section 7.6), E-R analysis for safety was not performed.

L1494: ## **Evaluation of Dosing Regimen**
L1495: The Applicant's proposed dosing regimen is based on (b) (4) body weight. (b) (4)

To estimate steady state PK profiles of levacetylleucine following the proposed dosing regimen, PopPK simulations were conducted using the reviewer's PopPK model, which is similar to the Applicant's. As body weight was the only significant covariate included in the PopPK model, simulations were conducted in virtual populations, each of which includes 1000 subjects whose body weight was sampled from uniformly distributed values within each body weight group. To generate the reference range for comparison, PopPK simulation was conducted based on a virtual NPC population (n=1000) sampled from the subjects with NPC from Trials IB1001-201 and IB1001-301. The red lines in Figure 28 to Figure 30 present 5th, mean, and 95th percentiles of the reference PK exposures (C_{max} and daily AUC).
L1496: The simulations show that PK exposure metrics (C_{max} and daily AUC) are generally similar across body weight groups 15 to &lt;25 kg (dose group 3), 25 to &lt;35 kg (dose group 4), and \ge 35 kg (dose group 5). The proposed dosing regimens for patients weighing \ge 15 kg are deemed adequate and supported by the efficacy and safety findings from Trial IB1001-301, and the similar PK exposures across body weight groups.

L1506: ## 16. Efficacy
L1507: ## 16.1. Trial IB1001-201
L1508: ## 16.1.1. Criteria To Select Primary Anchor Test for CI-CS
L1509: The procedures for selecting the primary anchor test were defined in an administration and scoring manual for clinical impression of change in severity (CI-CS) Version 3, dated January 21, 2020. At Visit 1, the Investigating Physician selected either the 8-meter walk test (8MWT) or the 9-Hole Peg Test of the dominant hand (9HPT-D) for each subject based on the subject's performance at Visit 1 on the 8MWT, the Gait subtest of the SARA, the 9HPT-D, and a cognitive assessment. Examples of standard cognitive assessments include the Montreal Cognitive Assessment or the Mini-Mental State Examination.
L1510: The default selection of the 8MWT or 9HPT-D was based on the following criteria:
L1511: The 8MWT was selected as the default anchor test if the subject's:
L1512: - Score on the SARA Gait subtest was 2 to 7, or
- Time on the 9HPT-D was &lt;20 seconds or &gt;150 seconds.
L1513: The 9HPT-D was selected as the default anchor test if the subject's:
L1514: - Score on the SARA Gait subtest was 0, 1, or 8, or
- Time on the 9HPT-D was \ge 20 seconds and \le 150 seconds.
L1515: If the subject's score on the SARA Gait subtest was 2 to 7, and the time on the 9HPT-D was \ge 20 seconds and \le 150 seconds, the Investigating Physician had to determine whether improvement in functional aspect of gait or upper arm extremity were more clinically meaningful for the subject. In addition, when selecting the primary anchor test, the Investigating Physician could elicit from subjects and their family/caregivers which was their preferred or most relevant outcome for seeing improvement.
L1516: ## 16.1.2. Other Efficacy Endpoints
L1517: The key secondary endpoints for the parent study of Trial IB1001-201 were as follows:
L1518: - The individual components of the primary endpoint (CI-CS from Visit 2 to Visit 4 and CI-CS from Visit 4 to Visit 6).
- Difference in the blinded rater's Clinical Impression of Severity values from baseline to end of treatment and from the end of treatment to the end of washout.
- Measurement of change in performance of the CI-CS score of either the 9HPT-D or the 8MWT reclassified to a 3-point scale.
- An evaluation of the CI-CS for the test (9HPT-D or 8MWT) that was not selected as the primary anchor test.
L1519: Secondary endpoints for the parent study of Trial IB1001-201 were as follows:
L1520: - Spinocerebellar ataxia functional index (SCAFI)
- SARA
- European Quality of Life, 5-Dimension, 5-Level for subjects aged \ge 18; European Quality of Life, 5-Dimension, Youth for subjects aged 6 to 17 years (2 parts: the European Quality of Life visual analogue scale and the European Quality of Life descriptive system)
- Modified disability rating scale (mDRS)
- Clinical global impression scale of severity (CGI-S) by the treating physician; clinical global impression scale of severity by the caregiver; clinical global impression scale of severity by the subject
- Clinical Global Impression of Change by the treating physician; Clinical Global Impression of Change by the caregiver; Clinical Global Impression of Change by the subject
L1521: ## SARA
L1522: For the modified intent-to-treat population, the mean change in SARA total score during the treatment period (Visit 4 versus Visit 2) was -1.2 (SD: 2), indicating an improvement in SARA score with levacetylleucine. The mean change during the washout period (Visit 6 versus Visit 4) was 1.4 (SD: 2.6), indicating a deterioration in the subjects' condition at the end of the washout period.
L1523: ## 16.2. Trial IB1001-301: Other Efficacy Endpoints
L1524: ## SCAFI
L1525: The SCAFI is composed of a timed 8MWT to be carried out at maximum speed, the 9-Hole Peg Test (which evaluates both the dominant and non-dominant hand), and the performance auditory-perceptual assessment rate to evaluate speech performance. The 8MWT and 9-Hole Peg Test are measured in 0.1 second, and a lower time indicates a clinical improvement. The performance auditory-perceptual assessment test evaluates the severity of dysarthria, and higher score indicates a clinical improvement. After the assessment, raw scores were transformed into reciprocals and converted into subtest Z-scores. The final SCAFI was then generated as the arithmetic mean of all 3 Z-scores. Following the primary analysis method, the estimated treatment difference between levacetylleucine and placebo in SCAFI total score was 0.07 (95% confidence interval [CI]: 0.0, 0.15), indicating more improvement in the levacetylleucine treatment period.
L1526: ## mDRS
L1527: The mDRS consists of 6 domains (ambulation, manipulation, seizures, language, swallowing, and ocular movements) and the scale ranges from 0 to 24, where 0 is the best neurological status and 24 is the worst. The mDRS composite score is calculated as the sum of the 6 components divided by 24, so is on a 0 to 1 scale.
L1528: In the intent-to-treat population, the mean baseline mDRS composite score was 0.44 (SD 1.7) in the levacetylleucine-placebo sequence and was 0.52 (SD 0.11) in the placebo-levacetylleucine sequence. The estimated treatment difference using the primary analysis method was -0.02 (95% CI: -0.04, -0.00), indicating improvement in mDRS composite score with levacetylleucine compared to placebo.
L1529: ## 5DNPCCSS
L1530: The 5-domain Niemann-Pick disease type C clinical severity scale sum score is defined as a composite score of 5 major domains (ambulation, speech, swallow, fine motor skills, and cognition) in Niemann-Pick disease type C clinical severity scale. Scoring of each domain ranges from 0 to 5. A lower score indicates a clinical improvement. See Section 16.3 for a scoring form. The estimated treatment difference based on the mixed model for repeated measures used in the primary analysis was -0.3 (95% CI: -0.74, 0.15) for the mean change from baseline in 5-domain Niemann-Pick disease type C clinical severity scale total score (Table 86).

L1537: The estimated treatment difference using a two-sample t-test based on only Period I data was 0.34 (95% CI: -0.38, 1.06) favoring placebo. As shown in Figure 31, subjects experienced more worsening when receiving placebo or improvement when receiving levacetylleucine during Treatment Period II compared to Treatment Period I, and thus an overall treatment effect using within-subject comparison favored levacetylleucine.

L1540: **Table 87** shows the review team's analyses based on within-subject treatment differences in 58 subjects who completed both Visit 4 and Visit 6 assessments. Among the 58 completers, 22 (38%) subjects had a treatment difference favoring levacetylleucine, 11 (19%) subjects had a treatment difference favoring placebo, and 0 (43.1%) subjects had a treatment difference of zero score. The overall estimated treatment difference was -0.43 (95% CI: -0.86, -0.02) with a p-value of 0.08 based on a nonparametric sign test.

L1546: ## **Correlations Between SARA, mSARA, and fSARA Total Scores**
L1547: Both the functional scale for the assessment and rating of ataxia (fSARA) and the modified scale for the assessment and rating of ataxia (mSARA) are based on the modification to the original SARA. Using all available visits for 60 randomized subjects, the estimated Spearman's rank correlation coefficient (Spearman's \rho) between SARA total score and fSARA total score was 0.94 (p-value &lt;0.001). The estimated Spearman correlation between mSARA total score and fSARA total score was 0.89 (p-value &lt;0.001). The estimated Spearman correlation between mSARA total score and SARA total score was 0.98 (p-value &lt;0.001).
L1548: ## **Correlations Between SARA, mDRS, and NPC-CSS in Ambulation and Speech Domains**
L1549: The ambulation and speech domains of mDRS and NPC-CSS are measured in a similar manner to the SARA. **Table 88** demonstrates that the three instruments are significantly correlated in total and domain-specific scores among 58 subjects who completed Visit 2, Visit 4, and Visit 6 assessments.

L1594: ## 22. Data Integrity-Related Consults (Office of Scientific Investigations, Other Inspections)
L1595: Trial IB1001-301 was an adequate and well-controlled trial and Trial IB1001-201 provided data that served as CE. Three clinical investigators, Drs. Gascon-Bayarri, Bremova-Ertl, and Martakis, were inspected in this NDA. During the clinical investigator inspections, source records documenting the following were reviewed and verified against the Applicant's data line listings:
L1596: - For Protocol IB1001-201: for all 10 participants enrolled by Dr. Gascon-Bayarri, the treating physician's CI-CS scores for the 9HPT-D or 8MWT and the central blinded rater's CI-CS scores for 9HPT-D or 8MWT (i.e., primary efficacy endpoint).
- For Protocol IB1001-301: for all 12 randomized participants at Dr. Bremova-Ertl's site and for all 9 randomized participants at Dr. Martakis' site, the mean change of mSARA (i.e., primary efficacy endpoint) and the original SARA (i.e., key secondary efficacy endpoint).
L1597: No discrepancies were noted. However, a data reliability concern was noted in the Visit 6 mSARA and SARA assessment for Subject (b) (6) (enrolled in Protocol IB1001-301 by Dr. Bremova-Ertl). The investigator noted in this subject that the mSARA/SARA was performed
L1598: remotely over videoconference using “WhatsApp” for Visit 6. In addition, the investigator performed the assessment on [REDACTED] (Visit 6) but did not document the assessment and scores until [REDACTED]. The investigator also made multiple changes to the individual assessment domains on [REDACTED] (see Clinical Inspection Summary for details).
L1599: A sensitivity analysis is recommended regarding the Visit 6 assessment for the mSARA, and SARA data generated from Subject [REDACTED]. Notwithstanding the above concern with how the data were documented for Subject [REDACTED] in Protocol IB1001-301, the trials appear to have been conducted adequately. The study data, including the primary and key secondary efficacy endpoint data for the two trials (i.e., Protocols IB1001-201 and IB1001-301), generated by these three sites appear acceptable in support of the respective indication.
L1600: The review team concluded that inspection of these three clinical investigators was sufficient to assess the data quality and integrity. The Office of Scientific Investigations concluded that, other than the issues described above, the clinical data collected at the three sites appeared reliable. No significant trial conduct issues or regulatory violations were identified at any of the three sites.
L1601: See the Clinical Inspection Summary dated July 24, 2024 for additional details.

L1761: Figure 4: Study Design, Naïve Subjects, Trial IB1001-301. The diagram illustrates a crossover trial design. The study consists of a Baseline period (Weeks -2 and -1), followed by two treatment periods (Period I and Period II, each 3 months long). Period I is 12 weeks (Weeks 1-12) and Period II is 12 weeks (Weeks 13-24). The trial is divided into two sequences. Sequence 1 (N=30) receives IB1001 during Period I and Placebo during Period II. Sequence 2 (N=30) receives Placebo during Period I and IB1001 during Period II. The crossover occurs between Period I and Period II. The timeline shows visits corresponding to Baseline, Period I, and Period II, including the start of intervention and the crossover point.
L1762: Figure 6 is a line graph showing the Mean fSARA Total Score (Y-axis, ranging from 4 to 7) over time (X-axis: Baseline, Visit 3, Visit 4, Visit 5, Visit 6) for two treatment sequences. The solid blue line represents Sequence 1 (levacetylleucine treatment period), and the dashed gray line represents Sequence 2 (placebo treatment period). Error bars represent standard error (SE). At Baseline, Sequence 1 is approximately 5.3 and Sequence 2 is approximately 6.3. Sequence 1 decreases steadily to approximately 4.3 at Visit 4, then increases slightly to approximately 4.8 at Visit 6. Sequence 2 decreases slightly to approximately 5.8 at Visit 4, then increases to approximately 5.9 at Visit 6.

L1769: Figure 16A is a bar chart showing Total GSL (pmol/mg) at 59 days of age in the Forebrain of mice. The Y-axis ranges from 0 to 3,000,000 pmol/mg. The X-axis shows different mouse groups: Npc1+/+ UT (blue), Npc1-/- UT (red), Npc1-/- ADLL (green), Npc1-/- ALL (purple), Npc1-/- ADL (orange), and Npc1-/- MIG (black). The Npc1-/- UT group shows the highest GSL levels (around 2,500,000 pmol/mg). The Npc1+/+ UT group shows the lowest GSL levels (around 500,000 pmol/mg). The chart indicates a significant difference (p < 0.0001) between the Npc1+/+ UT and Npc1-/- UT groups. Percent changes relative to the Npc1-/- UT group are shown: ADLL (-3%), ALL (-15%), ADL (-6%), and MIG (-15%).

The Npc1+/+ UT group shows the lowest GSL levels (around 500,000 pmol/mg). The chart indicates a significant difference (p < 0.0001) between the Npc1+/+ UT and Npc1-/- UT groups. L1771: Figure 17 displays gait analysis results comparing untreated wildtype (Npc1^{+/+} UT) and untreated Npc1^{-/-} mice with Npc1^{-/-} mice treated with N-acetyl-L-leucine (NALL) either pre-symptomatically or symptomatically.
A. Sequential gait cycle: Shows gait analysis changes in the y coordinate over time (weeks) for untreated wildtype (Npc1^{+/+} UT, blue), untreated Npc1^{-/-} (red), and Npc1^{-/-} treated with ADL (acetyl-D-leucine) or ALL (acetyl-L-leucine) starting at 8 weeks (green, dark blue, orange) or 3 weeks (purple, light blue, brown). The graph is divided into Symptomatic Treatments (8 weeks start) and Pre-Symptomatic Treatments (3 weeks start). Untreated Npc1^{-/-} mice show significant gait abnormalities (large negative y coordinate changes). NALL treatment, especially when initiated pre-symptomatically (3 weeks start), significantly reduces these abnormalities, bringing treated Npc1^{-/-} mice closer to the wildtype baseline (dashed line).
B. Standard Error of the Means (SEMs) of Y coordinate changes: Shows the SEM of Y coordinate changes (mean \pm SD, n=6) for the groups in Panel A. Untreated Npc1^{-/-} mice (red) show the highest SEM (around 3.0). Untreated wildtype mice (blue) show the lowest SEM (around 1.0). NALL treatment, particularly pre-symptomatic treatment (3 weeks start, purple and light blue), significantly reduces the SEM compared to untreated Npc1^{-/-} mice (p<0.003 and p<0.0001 respectively, indicated by asterisks).
L1772: Figure 18A displays box plots showing the Duty cycle of gait (%) at 8 weeks for Npc1^{-/-} mice treated pre-symptomatically. The Y-axis ranges from 20% to 100%. L1780: Figure 21 is a line graph showing the Mean Body Weights (BW) of Rabbits in the Embryo-Fetal Development Study, Phase 1, Means, plotted against Days (5 to 30). The Y-axis represents BW (g), ranging from 3,500 to 3,800. The X-axis represents Days, ranging from 5 to 30. Four groups are compared, based on the dose administered:
F / 0 mg/kg/day (Control, Blue line with triangles)
F / 675 mg/kg/day (Green line with triangles)
F / 1250 mg/kg/day (Orange line with triangles)
F / 2500 mg/kg/day (Red line with triangles)
The graph illustrates that body weight generally increases over the study period. The control group (F / 0 mg/kg/day) shows the highest body weight, peaking around 3,750 g at Day 30. The 2500 mg/kg/day group shows the lowest body weight, peaking around 3,600 g at Day 15. All groups show fluctuations in body weight, particularly between Days 15 and 20.
L1781: Figure 22 is a line graph showing the Mean Food Consumption (FC) in Rabbits in the Embryo-Fetal Development Study, Phase 1, Means, plotted against Days (5 to 30). The Y-axis represents FC (g/animal/day), ranging from 50 to 150. The X-axis represents Days, ranging from 5 to 30. Four groups are compared, based on the dose administered:
F / 0 mg/kg/day (Control, Blue line with triangles)
F / 675 mg/kg/day (Green line with triangles)
F / 1250 mg/kg/day (Orange line with triangles)
F / 2500 mg/kg/day (Red line with triangles)
The graph illustrates that food consumption generally decreases over the study period, with the 2500 mg/kg/day group showing the most significant reduction, especially between Days 6 and 10, where consumption drops sharply to near 60 g/animal/day.
L1782: Figure 23 is a box plot titled "Effect of Meal Intake on Drug Concentration" showing Drug Concentration (\mu\text{g}/\text{mL}) versus Meal intake scenario (0 - without meal, 1 - with meal) at four different time points: 0.5 H, 1 H, 1.5 H, and 2 H. The Y-axis ranges from 0 to 15 \mu\text{g}/\text{mL}.
The plot is divided into four panels corresponding to the time points:
0.5 H: The 'Without meal' group (0) shows a median concentration around 7 \mu\text{g}/\text{mL}. The 'With meal' group (1) shows a median concentration around 2 \mu\text{g}/\text{mL}.
1 H: The 'Without meal' group (0) shows a median concentration around 7 \mu\text{g}/\text{mL}. The 'With meal' group (1) shows a median concentration around 2 \mu\text{g}/\text{mL}.
1.5 H: The 'Without meal' group (0) shows a median concentration around 7 \mu\text{g}/\text{mL}. The 'With meal' group (1) shows a median concentration around 2 \mu\text{g}/\text{mL}.
2 H: The 'Without meal' group (0) shows a median concentration around 7 \mu\text{g}/\text{mL}. The 'With meal' group (1) shows a median concentration around 2 \mu\text{g}/\text{mL}.
The box plots illustrate that the distribution of drug concentration is similar between the 'Without meal' and 'With meal' groups across all time points, supporting the conclusion that meal intake does not significantly affect drug concentration during the first 2 hours postdose.
L1783: Figure 24 displays four panels (A, B, C, D) illustrating the Goodness of Fit (GoF) plots for the Applicant's Final PopPK Model.
Panel A: IPRED, DV vs IVAR
Y-axis: IPRED, DV (log scale, 0.01 to 10). X-axis: IVAR (0 to 10). Shows observed concentrations (CObs) and predicted concentrations (IPRED) plotted against the independent variable (IVAR). The data points are clustered around the diagonal line, indicating a good fit.
Panel B: DV vs IPRED
Y-axis: DV (0 to 20). X-axis: IPRED (0 to 10). Shows observed concentrations (DV) plotted against individual predicted concentrations (IPRED). The data points are clustered around the diagonal line, indicating a good fit.
Panel C: CWRES vs PRED
Y-axis: CWRES (conditional weighted residuals, -4 to 4). X-axis: PRED (predicted concentration, 0 to 10). Shows CWRES plotted against PRED. Horizontal reference lines are drawn at y=2, y=0, and y=-2. The residuals are randomly scattered around the zero line, indicating a good fit.
Panel D: CWRES vs Standard Normal Quantiles
Y-axis: CWRES (conditional weighted residuals, -4 to 4). X-axis: Standard Normal Quantiles (Standard Normal Quantiles, -4 to 4). Shows CWRES plotted against Standard Normal Quantiles. The data points closely follow the diagonal line, indicating that the residuals are approximately normally distributed.
L1784: Figure 25 displays two Visual Predictive Check (VPC) plots comparing observed concentrations (CObs) against predicted concentrations (DV0) for two dose levels: 1 gram (Dose = 1 g) and 2 grams (Dose = 2 g). The x-axis represents the independent variable (IVAR), and the y-axis represents the dependent variable (DV, DV0).
The left plot (Dose = 1 g) and the right plot (Dose = 2 g) show observed quantiles (ObservedQuant...) and predicted quantiles (PredictedQuant...) for 5%, 50%, and 95% percentiles. The observed data points (DV vs IVAR) are scattered around the predicted median (50% line), generally falling within the 5% to 95% prediction intervals, indicating a good fit of the model to the observed data.

L1786: Figure 27 is a scatter plot illustrating the Exposure-Response relationship for the functional Scale for the Assessment and Rating of Ataxia (fSARA). The X-axis represents Daily AUC (\mu\text{g}\cdot\text{h}/\text{mL}), ranging from 20 to 50. The Y-axis represents fSARA (Active-Placebo), ranging from -3 to 1. The plot displays data points categorized by Daily dose (2 g, 3 g, 4 g) and Age group (<10yo, \ge 10\text{yo}). Black points represent the mean, and error bars represent \pm standard error of the mean. The data points are widely scattered, showing no clear dose-response or age-response relationship for the fSARA endpoint.
L1787: Figure 28 displays two boxplots comparing simulated pharmacokinetic (PK) exposures across five dose groups (1 to 5).
The left plot shows the maximum plasma concentration (C_{max}) in \mu\text{g/mL}. The right plot shows the Daily Area Under the Concentration-Time Curve (Daily AUC) in \mu\text{g}\cdot\text{h}/\text{mL}.
For both plots, the whiskers represent the 5th and 95th percentiles of the simulated exposure metrics. The box represents the 25th, 50th, and 75th percentiles. Red lines indicate the 5th, mean, and 95th percentiles of the reference PK exposure.

L1788: Figure 31: Mean \pm SE Plot of 5DNPCSS Total Score Over Time per Treatment Sequence. The plot shows Mean 5DNPCSS Total Score (Y-axis, 9 to 13) versus Visit (X-axis: Baseline, Visit 4, Visit 6). Two treatment sequences are compared: Sequence 1 (solid lines, blue) and Sequence 2 (dashed lines, gray). Error bars represent standard error (SE). At Baseline, both sequences have a score around 10.5. At Visit 4, Sequence 1 is slightly lower (around 9.8) and Sequence 2 is slightly higher (around 11.8). At Visit 6, Sequence 1 is around 10.2 and Sequence 2 is around 11.5. The overall trend suggests that Sequence 2 scores are higher than Sequence 1 scores at Visit 6.

L1792: Figure 35 is a Dumbbell Plot showing the Change in fSARA speech disturbance item (X-axis) for each Subject ID (Y-axis). The plot compares the change from baseline to end of treatment (Visit 4 or Visit 6) for subjects receiving Levacetylleucine (open circles) versus Placebo (diamonds). The X-axis ranges from -4 to 4, with 0 indicating no change. The plot shows that for most subjects, the change is near zero, indicating minimal difference between the two treatment periods. A few subjects show a positive change (improvement) in the Levacetylleucine period compared to the Placebo period, and one subject shows a large positive change (improvement) in the Placebo period.
